## Ad hoc

- [x] **T122 — status view start-open configuration.** User-directed (2026-11-25): expose configuration so a status view can start with everything OPEN (sections expanded at full detail), potentially as the default for old tests, while preserving fast time-to-initial-buffer — diffs must load lazily and refresh into the buffer as they become available. Scouted (base f83e73c4) and advised; design below is worker-ready for @planner (TICKET).
  - Facts (VERIFIED): deferred open seeds `level=LEVEL_OPENING(1)` + `default_visibility()` (collapses the three sections) + `auto_expand=true` at `StatusView::loading_with_branch` (src/status_view.rs:177-186); a fast acquisition beating the 500ms OPEN_PROMISE auto-expands to `LEVEL_MAX` via `install_timed_hunks` (:286-302); refresh re-seeds nothing (visibility/level persist per view instance). Config never reaches StatusView: it must be threaded from the `view_execution.rs` open site into `open_status_loading` (Session holds no config handle; Shared does). A parsed-but-dead config key `sections.default_visibility` already exists (src/config.rs:144-150, :626-627) — the natural carrier, satisfies the no-shims rule.
  - Advisor design: (1) revive `sections.default_visibility` as an enum `auto` (default, current behavior) | `open` (seed `Visibility::new()` so unknown=expanded AND set the detail level — visibility alone does nothing at LEVEL_OPENING; pick the level so `open` matches what the sync `Session::open` path produces) | optionally `collapsed` (no auto-expand). Pass a small value object (visibility default + level) from view_execution into loading_with_branch; never pass a ConfigService handle. (2) Read the config at each view open (per-instance is sufficient; no "first status appearance" gating — visibility already persists across refresh, and a session-construction-time freeze would ignore later config changes). (3) In `open` mode seed `auto_expand=false` so `install_timed_hunks` cannot rewrite the seeded state; the :910 user-override-clears-auto_expand rule stays `auto`-only. A user collapse during streaming must win over arriving hunks (falls out if render respects visibility). (4) Tests: keep `auto` as the product default (the ~dozen collapsed-count assertions in tests/status_render.rs:254-279, tests/server_async.rs:989-997, tests/navigation.rs:632-638, clients/vscode/test/*.js, tests/ui/scenarios/status-basics.scn stay honest unchanged); most old sync-path tests are already expanded via `Session::open`. New permanent tests: `open` with fast acquisition (first render complete), `open` with slow acquisition (headers/file rows first, hunks arrive by incremental edit), and a refresh during streaming discarding stale hunk results by generation.
  - FETCH MODEL (VERIFIED by scout, base f83e73c4, resolving the earlier caveat): hunk fetch is inline in render — `render_tree` (src/status_view.rs:392-417) synchronously calls `repo.file_hunks_with_origins` per expanded file on cache miss (:403-405), the whole render runs inside `spawn_blocking` under the session mutex + repo lock. OPEN_PROMISE=500ms bounds the WHOLE deferred batch (view_execution.rs:432-436 computes `remaining` and times out the entire `fetch_timed_hunks`), not per-file; `timed_fetch` (:237-251) is only the work list; there is no per-render budget. The only background prefetch is `spawn_timed_hunks` (:414), armed only while `auto_expand` is true. The synchronous `open_repo` path (LEVEL_MAX, implicit-expanded) ALREADY fetches every diff inline today — Plain bootstrap (plain.rs:111-117) and old tests via `Session::open` use it. On the deferred path's slow case today (INFERRED, planner confirms): the timed-out batch never installs, sections stay collapsed, nothing expands later — check whether partial batch results are cached or discarded.
  - ADVISOR REVISED DESIGN (after the scout): mechanism is (b) reuse the timed-batch machinery — reject inline fetch on the rich path (holds the session mutex + repo lock across every diff; all keys/renders stall on large worktrees) and defer the full streaming redesign, taking only its slice: while a batch is in flight, render must not fetch files the batch owns. `open` mode: fast → batch beats promise, `install_timed_hunks` gives a complete first buffer (today's behavior); slow → publish sections expanded at LEVEL_MAX with file rows only, treat in-flight files as pending in render, let the batch finish with NO timeout, install hunks with one re-render diffed against the acked version. Refresh bumps generation and drops a stale batch; a user collapse before install wins (render respects visibility). EXISTING TRAP to fix in the same ticket: after the fast path `auto_expand=false`, so `arm_refresh_fetch` does not re-arm and a refresh of an expanded view already refetches every diff inline under the lock today.
  - TEST SYNCHRONIZATION (advisor, replacing the "variable or mock to block" idea): make OPEN_PROMISE an injectable parameter passed down from `Shared` to the timed-fetch site — production keeps 500ms; tests needing a complete first buffer set it large-but-finite (e.g. 10-30s — this IS the user's "block until diffs are available", no mock, no second code path — no-test-only-paths rule holds; DO NOT use `Duration::MAX`: the `tokio::time::timeout(remaining, fetched)` in spawn_timed_hunks (view_execution.rs:432-433) would become unbounded, so a stall outside the per-git-command deadline path hangs the test instead of falling into the slow path; per-file failures are safe — fetch_timed_hunks treats them as warn+empty, status_view.rs:260-271); tests needing the slow path set it `ZERO`. Precedent for an explicit gate when a test must observe the in-flight state: `FirstStatusGate` (scripts/ui_lib/fixtures.py:22) holds acquisition open for UI scenarios — imitate that shape (or the `HunkFetchGate` barrier below) rather than stretching the promise. Only if a test must observe the pending in-flight state, add an optional `HunkFetchGate` barrier the batch waits on before installing, passed as a constructor argument through the same `Shared` seam (integration tests don't compile with cfg(test); no env vars or config keys). Do NOT mock `file_hunks_with_origins` — real fixture repos are the better oracle and the backend boundary has no mock implementation. Native/VSCode harnesses can't inject Rust values: expose a hidden server flag `--open-promise-ms` for those fixtures.
  - TODAY'S TEST WAITS (what new tests should reuse/pin): sync-path tests are deterministic by construction; tests/ui/scenarios/status-basics.scn is a HIDDEN 500ms RACE (passes only if acquisition beats the promise — pin with MAX); tests/server_async.rs:989-997 `!contains "@@"` likely racy (INFERRED — pin with ZERO if the fixture can auto-expand); VSCode `waitForText` is eventual-poll-with-timeout (fine as oracle, but pin via the flag); tests/navigation.rs:632-638 explicit show-all, not racy. New tests should await rendered-version notifications / generation counters, extending the existing next-render/ack helper in tests/server_async.rs rather than text polling.
  - test: config value reaches `loading_with_branch` and `open` seeds expanded sections + matching level with `auto_expand=false` (fast and slow acquisition variants). test: `auto` behavior unchanged (existing collapsed-count assertions). check: Neovim/VSCode clients need no change to honor the option (they render server text verbatim), confirmed by one targeted scenario or recorded as an assumption with rationale.
  - Done: 06eb55d (config enum + deferred seeding, then the streaming half: seeded-open `timed_fetch` is unbounded, `spawn_timed_hunks` bypasses both promise gates, the render gate never inline-fetches while a batch owns the hunks, open-mode install keeps the seeded visibility/level, and only a command `common.refresh` re-arms; evidence note updated)
  - Verified: `make test-rust FILTER="--test status_render"` → `ok: rust` (19 passed, `build/test-logs/rust.ZbudJI`) and `make test-rust FILTER="--test server_async"` → `ok: rust` (75 passed, `build/test-logs/rust.pgv9DE`), both @ 06eb55d; the open-mode slow/refresh/collapse/fast server tests and the view-level superseded-generation test are green, and the `auto` tests are unchanged

Single `build` prerequisite completes before fan-out and the fan-out sub-makes pass `-o build`, so no two Cargo invocations overlap; per-suite logs stay unique via the `run` macro's `mktemp build/test-logs/$(1).XXXXXX`. Failure propagation: the recipe collects every child pid (`pids="$$pids $$!"`) and waits on each in turn (`wait $$pid || status=1`), so any one failing group sets `status=1` and the recipe `exit $$status` fails the target (a bare `wait` would have returned only the last child's status).

Timed-promise semantics from the user: a background diff fetch that completes within ~500 ms of the section publish opens its diffs by default; otherwise the fetch is cancelled and the section stays closed until explicitly opened on demand. All work runs inside deferred acquisition after the early publish, so this improves time-to-full-status, not head-visible time. Keep hunk identity path+content (never by index), server-owned fold state and the two-phase lifecycle untouched. test: state/rendering and conformance coverage asserts closed default, the ~500 ms open-or-cancel boundary (both sides) and on-demand fetch serving a later open. check: native text/structured evidence confirms closed sections on open in the real UI.

## Bugfix

## Audit findings and work order (2026-11-18)

This file alone owns checkbox states and Done evidence. [TASKS_PART_SEVEN.md](./TASKS_PART_SEVEN.md) owns detailed numbered scopes/dependencies, not duplicate completion status. Current contracts live in maintained architecture/protocol references, not historical attempt logs.

Recommended order (not execution authorization): reopened P1 Review; ready independent work; blocked prerequisites; gated features/release; optional/unscheduled work; completed ledger. Revalidate old export inventories before deletion. T105 has one Phase 6A scope; T111 routes menu/fold and minimum-Git decisions to T113/T119. T065/T066/T078 may overlap delivered work, but overlap is INFERRED, not completion; compare remaining acceptance when scheduled.

Policy decisions: release does not require zero hygiene debt; findings must instead be fixed/deleted, excepted with a concrete reason, or assigned an exact owner task, with missing tools disclosed honestly. Readable prose/source size is maintainability guidance, not an absolute byte gate; intact logs/program/wire transcripts need not be split solely for size. Before project version 1.0, breaking protocol/API/config changes are allowed without compatibility shims or additive-only guarantees; the rich wire's `v=1` label does not itself promise stability. Negotiation and validation invariants remain. T022 remains unchecked pending its actual evidence, finding dispositions and review; explicit human release go/no-go remains mandatory. Optional prototypes, stable freeze and post-release features are not silently added to the release prerequisite subset.

Provenance: **Base** means immutable `a366409749edf0e5591dff34d70500b73ab595b9:TASKS.md`. Ledger `B<number>` maps a retained checkbox occurrence to its exact base line, including its original title, nesting, full acceptance, verification and correction context. Historical closed prose and superseded gates are consolidated into that source, not another archive. `Refs` are historical references, not inferred implementation Done commits. Ambiguous `Done: containing commit` and source-only evidence remain ambiguous; no verification SHA is substituted for implementation evidence. Separate occurrences remain separate ledger rows, even when they concern the same umbrella ID. No unchecked record is consolidated into a checked one.

Durable risks: R-EDIT-REAL's parent is not green (T104 covers only a subset); last-window exit, dirty shutdown, cross-build, reload, workspace and platform acceptance remain. Binary files remain whole-file-only; T093 layout remains open. R-PLAIN-EDIT-EOF-RISK retains intermediate-echo/terminator uncertainty; R-T020-STALE's original cause is unknown, and its investigation authorizes no retry. T007.1n's native timeout followed by a diagnostic pass is not a proven fix. Linux-native evidence never establishes Windows/macOS behavior. T004.5c has no producing-command proof for multiChoice. Scripted models are not native/real-server conformance. The initial T021 Helix smoke failed commit/abort; later attachment/lifecycle evidence is scoped to R-T021-ATTACH/DRAFT, not universal Helix support.

Historical gates (details in Base): Phase 2 `5da5d86`/`ebf9ed1`; Phase 3 `b4dae94`; schema `92c78fb`; Phase 4 `84fb4bd`; regression pass `43318a2`. Superseded failed gates, deleted success logs, missing-tool reports and bounded reviews are provenance, not current instructions or evidence that unrun suites passed. Current T022 evidence and holds are below.

Historical reference mapping (not additional tasks or completion claims): R-VS/A06 identifies the native routing boundary in Base line 98; T008.8 names the native-pick emitter grouping at lines 165–166; IMM-6 remains part of T008.9's recorded contract at line 177; T008.9c names the pick grouping used by T008.9d/e at lines 198–201. R-4.1 and R-4.1a are the old fixup/squash references at line 722, with instant autosquash routed to T019 at line 851; R-5.1 is the retained registry availability reason at line 1214. T120 is the formatter umbrella at lines 1185 and 1813–1816; its child records remain in the ledger.

The generated test binary lives under `target/debug/build/magit-lsp/<hash>/out/`, so the fallback (`current_exe` → two parents up → profile dir), introduced by the test-support extraction `fd4625f`, assumes the `target/debug/<bin>` layout and resolves the wrong directory; Linux is unaffected because the Makefile passes `MAGIT_LSP_BIN` explicitly and bare-cargo Windows/macOS lanes hit the fallback. Windows/macOS CI evidence is a recorded follow-up, not locally claimable (Linux-only verification policy).

### Robustness — Deep dive on stale operations, freezes and hangs

Verified merge gate (2026-11-21): `make test` passed at `f83aa14` (`build.r88zib`, `rust.pZ5y5Z`, `ok: lua`, VSCode/typecheck/keybindings all green). Native VSCode A06 also passed at `601ff1c`; earlier hygiene was report-only exit 0 with recorded advisories. Safe merge preflight 1 deferred: main advanced to `c12ca5a` in another session and has dirty `TASKS.md`/`tests/test_magit_lsp.lua` plus untracked files. No merge, stash, reset or modification of main was attempted. Continue worktree and retry between later completed tickets.

Audit disposition (2026-11-21): reviewed historical incidents, server reader/request completion, lock/await boundaries, prompt/draft continuations, render delivery and stale-origin validation, shutdown/process cleanup, and native-client request lifetimes. One reachable open-workflow defect was fixed: VSCode first-render settlement on removal/disconnect (`2c714cf`), deterministic `make vscode-test` regressions plus native A06 `make vscode-e2e` evidence (`601ff1c`); review found no further actionable defect. This is not a blanket hang-free guarantee; full-suite merge gate is still separate.

Additional Neovim ownership evidence: `requests.lua:21-23,57-60,209-219` keys queues/running state by originating client ID; `init.lua:417-421` exit calls edit disconnect but does not clear those queues. Missing-client queued work terminates (`requests.lua:67-75`), while already-running work relies on its response callback (`:108-159`). No cross-client reopen blockage is demonstrated because fresh clients have distinct IDs; retained old-client state is a resource-cleanup risk, not evidence of a live server waiter (server EOF closes waiters). Native prompt callbacks preserve originating client ownership (`prompt.lua:20-37,168-186`, `pick.lua:20-30,43-70`; tests `tests/magit_lsp/menu.lua:479-524`, `tests/test_pick.lua:82-93`). Outstanding native widget teardown and arbitrary OS/transport timing remain explicit coverage limits, not presumed safe or reported as reproduced freezes.

Scope/evidence (2026-11-20): read-only targeted audit of queue history, `src/server.rs`, and reachable Plain ingress/completion around `src/server/plain.rs`, `src/server/view_execution.rs`, `src/session.rs` and `src/session/menu.rs`. This is not a whole-system robustness claim. Historical incidents separate into (a) actual reader starvation, fixed by RV-02 (`src/server.rs:1243-1255` historically; tracked completion and repository-turn ownership now required), (b) stale retained menu origin causing a refusal rather than a commit, fixed conservatively by `a8d6205` (`src/session/menu.rs`; do not infer positionality with `command_is_positional`), and (c) obsolete scenarios waiting for retired Ref/Remote `=u`/`=p` prompts, intentionally retired at `a7ed859`. The `plain_draft` timeout was not a deadlock: its fixture awaited a draft after the menu action correctly refused a stale Info target; truncated stderr concealed probes. The common.confirm concern was withdrawn because no reachable menu route invokes it. These are not open defects and must not be reintroduced as fixes.

Verified lifecycle invariant: outbound responses are correlated by ID and resolve the oneshot; timeout is 30 seconds, dropped receiver is a disconnect error, pending registration is RAII-cleaned, and terminal cleanup clears pending senders (`src/server.rs:339-386,661-699`). Actual draft applyEdit timeout/reject/error and close/recovery outcomes have direct regressions below; therefore the earlier generic P2 outbound-response ticket is withdrawn as redundant/unsupported, not left as a generic demand. In particular, an unanswered *user prompt* is intentionally user-waiting, not an outbound request timeout defect. No source-path hang was established in the traced routes.

### Deep-dive continuation — prompt, draft and delivery lifecycle (2026-11-20)

Traced reachable Plain document-prompt route: `plain_actions::menu.run-action` captures pending prompt while holding the session lock, releases it, then calls `drive_execution` (`src/server/plain_actions.rs:942-964`). `Execution::Prompt` either opens the document prompt (session lock scoped to `open_document_prompt`, `src/server/view_execution.rs:401-410`) or waits for native UI without holding that lock, then reacquires and resumes (`:434-451`). Plain `prompt.answer` validates the tree row, mutates under the session lock, releases it before driving the next step (`src/server/plain_actions.rs:980-995`). Closing prompt/origin cancels the continuation; origin close marks it invalidated, and `resume_prompt` drops invalidated/cancelled/missing-origin continuations without executing them (`src/session.rs:986-1045,2080-2096`). A stale prompt answer is rejected by current-row validation in `plain_actions.rs:980-995`; the earlier closed-origin regression and prompt lifecycle scenarios cover the relevant cancellation behavior (`tests/plain_draft.rs:656-689,1400-1490`; `tests/server_async/repository.rs:1485-1541`). No prompt-to-operation path was found holding the session mutex across an awaited client response.

Traced commit draft route: `dispatched_outcome` starts the detached repository operation after command/session locks have been released (`src/server/view_execution.rs:475-494`). It reserves deferred repository admission before creating the managed draft/progress and spawning the blocking Git worker; the worker owns the reservation and edit scope through the operation and publishes outcome only after dropping the edit (`:691-837,928-933`). Draft `workspace/applyEdit` is registered and awaited in a spawned runtime task, bounded by the configured 30-second delivery timeout; false/error/timeout cancels only the exact current unopened delivery (`src/server/plain.rs:1126-1139,1295-1325`). `showDocument` is independently bounded and on failure reports manual instructions (`:1328-1352`). Confirm/abort check run identity and synchronized document version before finishing/resolving (`src/server/drafts.rs:14-43,45-92`). Existing exact regressions cover reject/error/timeout before open and late failure after open (`tests/plain_draft.rs:2173-2285`), stale confirm (`:656-689`), abort/dirty-close (`:939-960,1400-1490`), and managed shutdown with pipelined late input (`tests/server_async/rich.rs:903-956`). These are concrete coverage; do not add duplicate tests.

Shutdown sets `closing` while serialized with progress publication, cancels outbound responders, closes Plain controller state, cancels operation tokens, aborts draft registry runs/scopes and closes prompt/pick waiters (`src/server.rs:661-699`); progress workers release through RAII and shutdown waits for progress completion (`:701-714`). Prompt/native answer paths resume with cancellation when prompt registry closes (`src/server/view_execution.rs:434-450`). This establishes the inspected lock/await ordering and cleanup path, not every native-client behavior. `workspace/applyEdit` rejection, error and timeout have direct draft regressions; Plain controller rejection/late response and URI-incarnation safety have `tests/plain_keys_exec.rs:450-510,667-720`.

Native-client and subprocess continuation (2026-11-20): VSCode `handlePrompt` defaults to cancellation, catches widget failures and sends exactly one `view/promptRespond`; empty secret input is cancellation (`clients/vscode/lib/requests.js:164-188`, `clients/vscode/lib/prompt.js:48-75`). Pick cancellation sends `cancelled:true, selected:[]` (`requests.js:251-288`). Existing vectors cover value semantics (`clients/vscode/test/test.js:485-559`), and the real VSCode integration drives a confirm response and pick cancellation (`clients/vscode/test/integration.js:492-559`); prompt cancel itself is indirectly captured by vectors, not an end-to-end server cancellation case. This is a concrete residual oracle gap, but no reachable defect is evidenced: server `resume_prompt(None)` cancels, and terminal cleanup closes prompt/pick channels (`view_execution.rs:434-450`, `server.rs:661-699`). Do not add a test unless it directly verifies this actual callback-to-server cancellation and terminal response behavior, not just duplicate the answer vectors.

VSCode first-render wait is a reachable client lifecycle defect, not a server lock hang: `ensureView` replays buffered renders synchronously when the descriptor arrives (`clients/vscode/lib/views.js:83-92`), while non-menu `showView` waits on `state.firstRender` before opening the virtual document (`:100-121`). Only successful full/edit application settles that promise (`clients/vscode/lib/requests.js:105-124`); initial resync returns without settling it (`:111-115`). `closeRoot` deletes view state and buffered render but does not settle firstRender (`views.js:225-243`). Server disconnect invokes `drafts.disconnect`, not view-open promise settlement (`clients/vscode/lib/session.js:259-261,291-298`; `clients/vscode/lib/drafts.js:813-823`), and `openStatus` awaits the opening promise with error handling only after it settles (`clients/vscode/lib/commands.js:77-95`). Thus a descriptor followed by close/disconnect before a successful full/edit render leaves the actual open workflow pending; later callbacks can also act against removed local state. Existing `clients/vscode/test/open.test.js:184-198` covers normal descriptor/render ordering, not close/disconnect or unsuccessful initial resync before first render. Add the focused task below; preserve normal early/late render behavior.

Other VSCode requests use language-client promises and catch/log failures (key/invoke warnings, prompt/pick log) (`clients/vscode/lib/requests.js:38-48,79-89,164-188,251-288`). Prompt/pick cancellation semantics have vector tests and real integration pick-cancel coverage (`clients/vscode/test/test.js:485-559`, `integration.js:492-559`); no distinct callback failure was established. Host close/reload/workspace lifecycle reaches disconnect and eventual process exit (`clients/vscode/test/e2e/real-file-lifecycle.mjs:38-114`), but is not a first-render waiter test. Do not infer this view-promise defect from those draft-focused shutdown cases.

Process cleanup: no orphan editor, server, extensionHost or Xvfb process from either run; the only `magit-lsp` process observed belonged to a separate concurrent Neovim UI run under `/home/william/src/magit-lsp` (different worktree/socket) and was left untouched. Neovim/client closure evidence is scoped in the existing native suite: `tests/ui/scenarios/close-reopen-views.scn` verifies stack/wipe/reopen; the prior record at `TASKS.md:84-87` says exact stack assertions and cleanup passed. That is not a systematic outstanding-prompt disconnect matrix. Server-side disconnect is explicit: EOF/read error invokes `terminal_cleanup` (`src/server.rs:1288-1299`), aborts editor registry entries and closes prompt/pick brokers (`:661-699`); handlers are joined and progress workers awaited before writer teardown (`:1304-1320`). Delayed Plain repair replies are guarded by URI incarnation and flight ID (`src/server/plain.rs:1445-1550`; tests `tests/plain_keys_exec.rs:450-510`), and draft delivery results by delivery identity/open state (`src/editor.rs:957-1010`; `tests/plain_draft.rs:2173-2285`). Existing exact tests mean no duplicate task for these paths.

Git cancellation is deliberately different from a timeout: the runner polls child process groups, sends TERM/CONT then KILL after grace, bounds pipe drain and reaping, and delegates a still-live leader to a reaper (`src/runner/owned.rs:108-128,161-230,293-339,362-397`). The command is not assigned an arbitrary short wall-clock timeout; normal Git and external editor work can await user input. Existing tests cover cancellation of a slow child, cancellation after child exit, and cancellation intent preservation (`tests/runner.rs:128-280`); managed draft lifecycle covers active helper abort/shutdown (`tests/plain_draft.rs:2173-2285`, `tests/server_async/rich.rs:903-956`). Residual assumptions are OS/process-group behavior and every failure point in real editor-helper startup; this source-only pass did not establish a concrete unbounded reachable subprocess hang.

Nested continuations: `Session::close` invalidates origin and resumes cancellation; `resume_prompt` consumes continuation without invoking it if invalidated, cancelled, or origin absent (`src/session.rs:986-1045,2080-2096`). Existing cancellation and dirty-close tests cover representative prompt/draft paths (`tests/server_async/repository.rs:1485-1541`; `tests/plain_draft.rs:1400-1490`). Exhaustive composition of multiple sequential prompts with repeated close/reopen is not separately covered, but no distinct reachable defect/test oracle was demonstrated; no ticket. Remaining bounded unknowns: arbitrary OS-level editor process behavior and unenumerated native transport failure timing. These are explicit residual assumptions, not a claim of universal robustness. No builds/tests/source changes were performed.

  - **Problem:** VERIFIED: the launcher replaces both HOME and XDG_CONFIG_HOME for Neovim and its server/Git descendants. INFERRED runtime consequences include failed commits and commits bypassing globally configured signing; these have not been exercised during review.

Preserve Git's original global-config lookup and precedence (both original XDG `git/config` and HOME `.gitconfig`, including existing `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` overrides); a temporary include-only global config is acceptable if needed, but do not copy secrets or flatten conditional includes. Isolation wording updated in `README.md`, `docs/CLIENT_INSTALL.md` and `clients/neovim/doc/magit.txt`. Part 2/2 (real-launcher Git subprocess identity/signing sentinel): the extended `LauncherSmokeTests` in `tests/release/test_client_setup.py` seeds a fixture HOME/XDG with `user.name`/`user.email` (set in both files to prove HOME wins Git precedence), `gpg.format` and `commit.gpgsign` from the original XDG `git/config`, plus `user.signingkey = SIGNING-SENTINEL-1234`; the launched editor's own `git config --get` (repository lookup, which follows includes as a commit does) must return every value. User-requested portable Nix release/CI work (source inspection `f838443`, 2026-11-20): execute the following ordered series before unrelated backlog. This is a plan, not artifact compatibility or hosted-run evidence. No tests/builds were run during review. The existing local artifact evidence proves execution inside the development environment only. Preserve protected untracked `.envrc`, `scripts/ci-local.sh`, `dist/` and all concurrent changes. No publication, tag, push, credentials or hosted dispatch is authorized. Every acceptance below is explicitly a permanent regression test or one-off check; execute commands serially through Make. Where a target is proposed, add it before running it. Native-platform unavailability is a recorded follow-up, not a reason to block independently verifiable Linux work.

- [x] **[P1] Reject symlink traversal for user excludes destinations** (`src/git.rs:98-110,1220-1238`, `user_excludes_path`, `GitRepo::add_ignore_rule`)
  - Done: `8c22e6b`; evidence/follow-up `e38ec69`, `1816f24`. Full backend verification follows with the adjacent excludes-lookup fix: the narrow `ignore` name filter does not select every excludes-named test.
  - **Problem:** Symlink-component rejection is only applied when the destination path starts under the repository root. `IgnoreScope::User` writes to the configured/default excludes path without checking it or its parent components, so a symlinked excludes file (or symlinked parent directory) redirects this append outside the selected file and can modify an unintended target.
  - **Why it matters:** Correctness and path safety — ignore writes must not follow attacker- or unexpectedly-controlled filesystem links merely because the destination is outside the worktree.
  - **Change:** Before creating parents or opening any ignore destination, apply a path-safety check to user-scope paths as well as repository paths. Resolve/check the destination's existing parent components and reject symlink components and a symlink destination; preserve normal creation of missing directories and append-only behavior. Add a Unix regression using a configured user excludes file symlink and a symlinked parent, asserting the operation errors and the link targets retain exact bytes.
  - **Done when:** **test:** `make test-rust FILTER="--test git_backend ignore"` proves both user-scope symlink cases are rejected without changing target bytes, while normal configured/default user excludes writes and existing repository scope tests still pass.
  - **Constraints:** Do not canonicalize a missing destination by following links, replace existing excludes content, or weaken repository-relative traversal validation.

- [x] **[P2] Propagate user-excludes configuration lookup failures** (`src/git.rs:98-110`, `user_excludes_path`)
  - Done: `25d2d30`; follow-ups `7e75553`, `713b4c3`. Verified: full `make test-rust FILTER="--test git_backend"` passed (`build/test-logs/rust.2Mf73L`), including configured/default excludes, linked worktree and both user-symlink rejection cases.
  - **Problem:** Every unsuccessful `git config --path --get core.excludesFile` currently falls through to the default path, although only the expected absent-key result should do so. A malformed/unreadable configuration or another Git failure can therefore cause a write to the wrong excludes file instead of reporting the lookup failure.
  - **Why it matters:** Correctness and error propagation — configured destination semantics must not silently degrade to a different user file when Git could not resolve configuration.
  - **Change:** Distinguish Git's expected missing-key status from other command failures and propagate the latter using the project's normal Git error representation; retain default-path resolution only for the absent-key case. Add focused coverage for missing key versus an actual configuration-read failure, asserting the latter creates or appends to no fallback file.
  - **Done when:** **test:** `make test-rust FILTER="--test git_backend ignore"` passes with an absent-key default-path case and a propagated configuration error case that leaves all exclude files unchanged.
  - **Constraints:** Do not change which effective `core.excludesFile` value Git selects or introduce a second config-resolution implementation.
  - Verified: `make test-rust FILTER="--test git_backend"` → `ok: rust (warnings detected; check log: build/test-logs/rust.2Mf73L)`; includes configured/default excludes, config lookup failure, linked worktree and symlink rejections.

- [x] **[P1] RV-07 — Propagate Git configuration read and clear failures** (`src/git.rs:908-996`, `branch_string_config`, `repo_string_config`, `set_branch_value`, `set_repo_value`)
  - **Problem:** Both config readers convert every Git command error into `None`, so permission, malformed-config and repository I/O failures are displayed as “unset.” Both unset writers also discard every `git config --unset-all` failure and return success, causing the command to report a successful clear when the repository config was not changed.
  - **Why it matters:** Correctness — configuration state must not be misreported or claim a write succeeded after Git rejected it; this also violates the existing user-facing Git error propagation contract.
  - **Change:** Distinguish Git's expected “key absent” result from other command failures for `branch_string_config` and `repo_string_config`, returning errors for failures other than absent keys. For each unset operation, ignore only Git's expected missing-key status and propagate all other failures. Preserve `--local` locality and the existing readable command error handling.
  - **Done when:** `test: make test-rust FILTER="--test git_backend --test commands"` covers absent keys as `None`, a config read failure as an error, and failed unset writes as errors without reporting successful configuration; existing local round-trip/clear tests pass.
  - **Constraints:** Do not change branch selection, supported config keys, public configuration behavior, or config scope; do not swallow errors with `.ok()` or ignored `run()` results.
  - Ordered parts: 1/2 backend config read/clear failure propagation and state regression tests (`make test-rust FILTER="--test git_backend"`); 2/2 command-path readable failure/no-success regression (`make test-rust FILTER="--test commands"`) and parent tick. Each part commits; backend tests are not rerun in Part 2 unless backend semantics change.
  - Verified Part 1: `make test-rust FILTER="--test git_backend"` passed; `configuration_read_failures_are_not_reported_as_unset` covers branch/repo read errors, and `failed_configuration_clears_preserve_values_and_absent_clears_succeed` covers exit-5 absent clears plus locked clears retaining values.

- [x] **[P1] RV-REL-01 — Validate branch protection with the available GitHub token permissions** (`scripts/check_release_automation.py:2176-2178`, `prepare_publication_runtime`; `.github/workflows/publish.yml:16-18`, `validate.permissions`)
  - **Problem:** Publication unconditionally calls `GET /branches/{branch}/protection`, whose fine-grained permission is Administration: read. The validation job's `GITHUB_TOKEN` has only contents/actions read, and workflow permissions cannot grant repository administration; protecting the branch and registering the publishers does not make this request authorized. A real run stops before downloading its handoff.
  - **Why it matters:** Correctness: the enabled publisher cannot complete with its declared credential contract.
  - **Change:** Replace this administrative endpoint dependency with a read-only branch metadata check supported by the declared token (including the branch's protected status), leaving detailed protection/ruleset configuration as an explicit maintainer prerequisite. Update the existing runtime API fixtures to model the actual response and permission boundary; do not treat a forbidden response as proof of protection.
  - **Done when:** `make release-automation-unit` covers protected/unprotected/missing branch metadata and proves the runtime never requests an Administration-only endpoint; record the relevant official endpoint permission reference without a live publication.
  - **Constraints:** No PAT, GitHub App credential, broader write permission, or silent protection-check bypass. Hosted execution remains pending.
  - Verified A1 (2026-11-22, `b8e8a04`): `prepare_publication_runtime` now reads `GET /branches/{default_branch}` (`scripts/check_release_automation.py:2174-2188`) and accepts only a 200 JSON object whose `protected` value is exactly `True`. The coherent branch fixture (`:3831`) still produces the successful handoff, while the six unconditional fail-closed fixtures (`:4115-4139`) reject HTTP 403, HTTP 404, `protected: false`, a missing `protected` field, `protected: "true"` and a non-object body. `make release-automation-unit` → `ok: release-automation-unit` (exit 0).
  - Verified A2 (2026-11-22): the self-test statically refuses any `/protection` request in `prepare_publication_runtime` (`:3857-3860`) and requires the coherent run's call log to contain `/branches/main` and no `/protection` path (`:4110-4114`). The same `make release-automation-unit` run kept every existing runtime/tag/artifact/VSIX/publisher fixture green; no fixture was weakened.
  - Verified A3 (2026-11-22): official endpoint permission references (docs.github.com, `apiVersion=2022-11-28`): "Get a branch" `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/branches/{branch}` requires **Contents: read** (or no authentication for public resources) at <https://docs.github.com/en/rest/branches/branches?apiVersion=2022-11-28>; "Get branch protection" `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/branches/{branch}/protection` requires **Administration: read** at <https://docs.github.com/en/rest/branches/branch-protection?apiVersion=2022-11-28>. `publish.yml:16-18` declares only `contents: read` (plus `actions: read` in `validate`), so the replacement endpoint is the only one the token supports. Detailed protection/ruleset rules are recorded as a maintainer prerequisite in `docs/RELEASING.md`. No live publication, tag, push, dispatch or credential was used; hosted execution remains pending.

Add a focused optional expected-locks input rather than checking out or executing the release source in the privileged workflow.

A2: `test_omitted_expectations_keep_checkout_lock_and_integrity_guards` (`:192`) keeps the matching-checkout pass, then rejects release-source locks and each single changed lock without `expected_locks`, with the existing transport, zip and provenance guards unchanged.
  - Part 2/2 (2026-11-22, base `0cf4853`, checker slice; impl `bec0d8b`): `scripts/check_release_automation.py:191-196` adds `SOURCE_LOCK_FILES`; `fetch_source_locks` (`:1731`) reads `Cargo.lock`, `flake.lock` and `clients/vscode/pnpm-lock.yaml` as inert base64 data through the read-only contents API at the bound commit and fails closed on non-200, wrong file identity/encoding, invalid base64 or size drift; `prepare_publication_runtime` (`:2376-2406`) peels the original tag to `source_sha` before manifest validation, supplies the fetched Cargo/flake digests to `validate_manifest_binding` and all three to `download_vsix_candidate.fetch_candidate(expected_locks=...)` (`:2461`), never reading the checkout locks or a default-branch ref.   - **Problem:** Existing-release acceptance discards all release metadata except asset names/digests. A2: creation-readback fixtures (`:4301-4320`) return 404, then `gh release create`, then a draft or wrong-tag body, and assert no registry command ran; the published-exact fixture (`:4321-4341`) completes `github-release, marketplace, openvsx` with one `vsce`/`ovsx` invocation over the relocated VSIX; an exact published release with conflicting digests is still refused (`:4284-4298`). A3: `publisher_command` (`:2809`) patches `subprocess.run` with ordered `gh`/registry responses so the real response parser, not a fake digest map, is exercised offline.

- [x] **[P1] RV-REL-05 — Report possible GitHub side effects when release creation fails** (`scripts/publish_release.py:324-327,382-385,421-425`, `ensure_release`, `CommandBackend.create_release`, `publish`)
  - **Problem:** Every failure in `ensure_release` is reported as `completed none; pending github-release, marketplace, openvsx`. `gh release create` creates the release before uploading assets, so an upload failure can leave a release and some assets behind; creation can also succeed and the readback fail. The current message incorrectly presents these cases as no external progress.
  - **Why it matters:** Correctness: honest partial-failure reporting is a release requirement, and the false clean-failure claim misdirects recovery after a partially created GitHub release.
  - **Change:** Distinguish failures before a mutation attempt from failures during creation or its verification. For the latter, report GitHub as partial/unverified and requiring inspection, while explicitly stating neither registry was attempted. Preserve confirmed completed destinations without claiming that an unsuccessful command rolled back its effects; no automatic retry is needed.
  - **Done when:** `make release-automation-unit` includes a backend that records a created release then raises on asset upload, and another that raises on post-create readback; both produce nonzero partial/unknown GitHub diagnostics and zero registry calls. A lookup failure before creation still reports that no mutation was attempted.
  - **Constraints:** Do not delete the partial release, overwrite assets, retry publication, or add a multi-registry recovery framework. Keep changes in the publisher and existing checker self-tests.
  - Implementation (2026-11-22, base `a7355b4`, impl `3a5b549`): `scripts/publish_release.py:60` adds `ReleaseMutationError(PublishError)`; `ensure_release` (`:336-343`) wraps `create_release` and the post-creation readback (including an exact-assets mismatch) so failures after creation was attempted are typed, while the pre-create lookup and existing-release conflict refusal stay ordinary `PublishError`; `publish` (`:455-462`) reports `partial or unverified ... require manual inspection ... neither registry was attempted` for the former and `no GitHub mutation was attempted` for the latter. Confirmed `github-release` completion, existing-release acceptance and the registry-lane `completed`/`pending` ordering are unchanged, and no rollback/retry path was added. A1: `scripts/check_release_automation.py:2768` gives `publisher_backend` `upload`/`readback`/`lookup` failure modes that record the release before failing; the loop at `:4251-4270` requires `partial or unverified`, `require manual inspection`, `neither registry was attempted` and the full pending list, forbids the no-mutation claim, and asserts a recorded `create_release` with zero `marketplace`/`openvsx` calls. A2: `:4272-4285` covers a pre-create lookup failure (`no GitHub mutation was attempted`, no `create_release`/registry), `:4386-4395` requires the same partial diagnosis for a real `CommandBackend` draft/wrong-tag readback mismatch, and `:4397-4430` covers an errored readback (HTTP 500 after creation → partial) versus a failed initial lookup (→ no mutation); `:4287-4306` drives `main()` through the real upload-failure path and requires exit status 1 plus a `partial or unverified` stderr diagnostic. A3: existing conflict refusal (`:4322-4325`), successful delivery ordering `github-release, marketplace, openvsx` (`:4440-4441`) and partial registry `completed github-release; pending ...`/`completed github-release, marketplace; pending openvsx` (`:4454-4466`) assertions still pass unchanged. A4: this queue tick with named evidence and the scoped implementation commit `3a5b549` (only `scripts/publish_release.py` and `scripts/check_release_automation.py`; no other path).
  - Verified: `make release-automation-unit` @ `3a5b549` → `ok: release-automation-unit` (exit 0), reusing the run against the unchanged source.

- [x] **[P1] STARTUP-RV-05 — Preserve refresh dirtiness newer than the held status completion** (`src/session.rs:346-374,818-827,851-860,2545-2565`, `OpenView::queue_refresh`, `OpenView::leave_text_mode`, `Session::refresh_view`; `tests/server_async.rs:75-176`, `status_open_text_mode_defers_held_completion_until_exit`)
  - **Problem:** `leave_text_mode` clears `dirty` whenever `pending_completion` is set, even when the refresh arrived after that completion was installed. A repository change followed by watcher/manual refresh during text mode is consequently discarded, and exit publishes the older acquired state. The comment and RV-01 implementation note claiming that a stored result supersedes a later refresh are incorrect.
  - **Why it matters:** Correctness: the last refresh request is lost and the status remains stale until another event; an earlier acquisition cannot include a subsequent filesystem change.
  - **Change:** Keep deferred completion publication separate from refresh dirtiness. Consume/reset the pending-completion marker on exit, but honor any queued refresh against the installed state before the single full replacement; preserve the no-extra-Git path when no newer refresh exists. Loading-time refreshes already use `queue_refresh_while_loading` and coalesced generations; do not discard later dirtiness to avoid redundant acquisition. Extend the existing text-mode completion regression with an explicit completion trace barrier, then create a new untracked file, invoke `common.refresh` while still in text mode, and exit. Also use the completion barrier in the held-error text-mode regression before its no-render assertion, rather than relying on `drain_notifications`' quiet interval. Correct the supersession assertion in the RV-01 implementation note without altering its historical completion record.
  - **Done when:** Both stored success and stored error remain unpublished and unversioned until exit; with no later refresh, success exit performs no additional status acquisition. With a refresh after completion and a new file, exit performs the needed refresh and emits one full replacement containing that file, with no stale base/edits. Run `make test-rust FILTER="--test server_async status_open"` and `make test-rust FILTER="--test text_mode"`; the later-refresh regression must fail against reviewed HEAD `45406ab3fad6b8db5fdceaf0be8ce38de1849cae`.
  - **Constraints:** Touch only `src/session.rs`, `tests/server_async.rs` and this queue. Preserve text-mode buffer ownership, loading-generation coalescing and close safety; do not fix this with unconditional refreshes, sleeps, client filtering or a new protocol field.
  - **Part 1 source (2026-11-22, reviewed HEAD `45406ab`):** `OpenView::leave_text_mode` (`src/session.rs:363-372`) now consumes `pending_completion` and honors `dirty` independently: a refresh queued after the stored completion runs `View::on_refresh` once before the single `RenderDelivery::Full`; with no later refresh it publishes the stored state without another Git read. The loading path still coalesces refreshes through `queue_refresh_while_loading`, and the completion install/fail paths and close safety are unchanged.
  - **Verified A1 (Part 1, test):** `make test-rust FILTER="--test server_async status_open"` — red at reviewed HEAD `45406ab` with the new regression and no source fix: exit 101, `status_open_text_mode_exit_honors_refresh_after_held_completion` failed at `tests/server_async.rs:273`, exit text omitted the file created after the held completion (`log build/test-logs/rust.LpIfXh`). After the source fix the same target exits 0 (`ok: rust`, 8 tests; `log build/test-logs/rust.ItAt1f`). The regression uses the completion trace barrier, creates `later-refresh.txt`, queues `common.refresh` in text mode, then asserts one full exit render at `version + 1` with no `baseVersion`/`edits`, containing both `status-open-text.txt` and `later-refresh.txt`, with `gate.invocation_count() == 2`.
  - **Verified A2 (Part 1, test):** In the same run, `status_open_text_mode_defers_held_completion_until_exit` now waits `wait_for_completion_barrier` and a resync round-trip before asserting no render/version change, and still requires `gate.invocation_count() == 1` when no later refresh arrives; `status_open_failure_is_terminal_and_loading_actions_are_safe` gained the trace barrier and the same round-trip before its no-render assertion. Both passed.
  - **Part 1 check (A3):** source, tests and this queue record are committed by explicit path with RV-05 left unchecked for Part 2; Part 2 owns the `--test text_mode` run and the final tick.
  - **Verified A1 (Part 2, check):** `make test-rust FILTER="--test text_mode"` → exit 0, `ok: rust`, `test result: ok. 6 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored` (warnings retained, log `build/test-logs/rust.9lME5q`). The existing text-mode suite exercises the Part 1 exit path without changed assumptions, so no source or test edit was needed and no assertion was weakened.
  - **Verified A2 (Part 2, check):** the same named target above passes against Part 1 source `5ccb0d8`; queue bookkeeping committed here. `Done:` names the Part 1 implementation commit.

- [x] **[P0] STARTUP-RV-01 — Defer loading completion delivery while text mode owns the buffer** (`src/session.rs:791-844`, `Session::install_fresh_status`, `Session::fail_fresh_status`; `lua/magit-lsp/view.lua:374-386`, `M.apply_render`)
  - **Problem:** At reviewed commit `d27e44a`, both completion paths render and advance the delivery version even when the user entered text mode during loading. The allowed `common.toggle-text-mode` command makes the buffer editable; the subsequent success/error render replaces those edits (or applies edits against the pre-edit loading text).
  - **Why it matters:** Correctness/data loss: text mode explicitly suspends server replacement until the user leaves it; ordinary refresh already respects this boundary.
  - **Change:** In the two Session completion methods, retain the generation-validated result without emitting text/edits or advancing the displayed version while the view is in text mode. Use the existing full-replacement-on-text-mode-exit lifecycle to expose the stored success/error then; do not trigger redundant synchronous Git solely to expose an already acquired result. Extend the existing held-status success/error tests in `tests/server_async.rs` to cover this boundary, replacing the current expectation that failure immediately renders in text mode.
  - **Done when:** With Git held, enter text mode and release either success or failure: no completion text/edits are delivered while text mode remains active; leaving text mode delivers the stored result as full text, with coherent versions and no stale ack base. Verify `make test-rust FILTER="--test server_async status_open"` and `make test-rust FILTER="--test text_mode"`.
  - **Constraints:** Preserve text-mode editing semantics, generation rejection and close safety; no client-side workaround, protocol change, or edits to the protected startup Lua helper. Source citations refer to the committed tree, not formatting residues.
  - **Part 1 source (2026-11-22, base `d27e44a`):** `Session::install_fresh_status`/`fail_fresh_status` install the generation-validated result, then detect `ViewMode::Text`, set a new `OpenView::pending_completion` flag and return without rendering, publishing text/edits or advancing the displayed version. `OpenView::leave_text_mode` consumes that flag, skips `on_refresh()` and renders the stored success/error with `RenderDelivery::Full`, so exit is a full replacement against a coherent (absent) base with no extra Git read when no later refresh arrived. The claim that a refresh queued after the stored completion is superseded by the same result was incorrect: that refresh was discarded, and STARTUP-RV-05 corrects exit to honor it. Close safety is unchanged: the live-generation lookup still returns `Ok(None)` before installation when the view is gone.
  - **Verified A1 (Part 1):** `make test-rust FILTER="--test server_async status_open"` → `ok: rust` (7 tests). Added `status_open_text_mode_defers_held_completion_until_exit` (held success: no render or version advance during text mode, resync still loading, exit full text/version+1/no base/no edits, `gate.invocation_count() == 1`, resync coherent) and reworked `status_open_failure_is_terminal_and_loading_actions_are_safe` (held failure no longer renders or advances version in text mode; exit delivers the stored error as full text).
  - **Verified A2 (Part 1, check):** scoped `status_open` target passed at base `d27e44a`; source, tests and these reviewer entries are committed here by explicit path. RV-01 stays unchecked until Part 2.
  - **Verified A1 (Part 2, test):** `make test-rust FILTER="--test text_mode"` → exit 0 (`ok: rust`; warnings retained, log `build/test-logs/rust.joXYOy`), 6 tests. `positional_requests_return_null_in_text_mode` now receives the deterministic loading render after the `view/open` response and waits for the deferred status completion render with `wait_for_view_render_after` (the `tests/server_async.rs:59-65,276` pattern) before asserting interactive symbols; all positional/null and text-mode entry/exit assertions are unchanged, with no sleeps and no source change. - [x] **[P1] STARTUP-RV-02 — Serialize status completion through repository publication ordering** (`src/server/view_execution.rs:156-212`, `finish_status_acquisition`; `src/server.rs:1691-1727`, `mutation_repo_path`; `tests/server_async.rs:39-120`, close-completion regressions)
  - **Problem:** Completion installs and versions its render under the session mutex, drops that mutex, then sends independently of the repository mutation turn. A refresh/close can execute and publish between installation and send, or completion can overtake a command's already-prepared notification. Rich delivery does not reject such stale renders; Neovim applies full text and assigns the older version. The close tests only query folding immediately after releasing Git, so they can pass before the deferred completion has even executed.
  - **Why it matters:** Correctness: newer state/mode can be overwritten by an older notification, and a closed view can receive a late render despite the claimed close-safe lifecycle.
  - **Change:** Keep acquisition outside session/global/repository locks, but admit its completion into the existing per-repository mutation ordering (`Shared::reserve_mutation`/`MutationTurn`) before installation. Retain the turn through live-generation validation, publication and enqueueing the render; follow the existing blocking-work dispatch pattern rather than blocking a Tokio worker on a contended session mutex. In `mutation_repo_path`, also order competing view lifecycle requests: currently `view/close`, `view/setMode` and non-stage `view/invoke` (including refresh) bypass turns, so adding a completion turn alone is insufficient. Preserve prompt-admission behavior. Reuse the same completion path for coalesced follow-ups. Extend the nearest async regressions to exercise completion competing with a refreshing command and close; establish an observable completion barrier before asserting absence of late output, not an unrelated folding response.
  - **Done when:** Deterministic interleavings cannot deliver an older completion after a newer render or close response, while the existing held-Git responsiveness and exact-base ack cases still pass. Close-success and close-error checks actually await completion processing and fail if the live-view check is removed. Verify `make test-rust FILTER="--test server_async status_open"`.
  - **Constraints:** Do not hold a mutation turn across slow acquisition, add a global serialization lock, weaken ack assertions, or rely on sleeps/client stale-render filtering. Keep this request/completion fix to `src/server.rs`, `src/server/view_execution.rs` and `tests/server_async.rs`; do not broaden it into a transport rewrite.
  - **Verified A1 (test):** `status_open_refreshes_while_loading_are_coalesced` now waits both completion traces, then records every post-loading `view/render` for the view in wire order and asserts exactly one render, at `loading_version + 1`, whose full text contains `overlap-fresh.txt` and not the loading text. A superseded completion publishing late would add a second render (older content at a newer version) and a duplicate would repeat the version, so the assertion covers versions, order and content rather than only absence of loading text. The wire-order capture lives in `tests/server_async.rs:338-392`.

The held-Git responsiveness, coalescing, RV-01 text-mode success/error and exact-base ack cases remain green; the close-success and close-error tests still wait `wait_for_completion_barrier` before asserting no render and a resync error. The source tree is unchanged except reconciled formatting, so the run remains valid.

- [x] **[P1] STARTUP-RV-03 — Account for resumed syscalls before ranking startup waits** (`scripts/profile_nvim_startup.py:84-130`, `parse_trace`; `docs/STARTUP_DIAGNOSIS.md:219-235`, syscall diagnosis)
  - **Problem:** `parse_trace` accepts only `pid timestamp syscall(` lines. With the configured `strace -f`, blocking calls commonly appear as an `<unfinished ...>` entry followed by `<... wait4 resumed> ... <duration>`; the duration-bearing resumed line is skipped. Counts include the entry but elapsed totals omit the wait, so the recorded conclusion that cost is not in `wait4` blocking is unsupported by this parser.
  - **Why it matters:** Correctness of measurement evidence: the omitted durations preferentially remove the blocking work this investigation is meant to measure.
  - **Change:** Handle unfinished/resumed records keyed by PID and syscall, count each call once, and attribute the resumed duration to that call. Detect incomplete pairs rather than silently presenting their durations as zero. Check successful exec results separately from failed PATH probes when reporting Git executions. Correct or explicitly invalidate the affected dated aggregates/conclusion; preserve raw historical provenance rather than silently relabeling old numbers.
  - **Done when:** A recorded parser check through the maintained profiling entry point shows interleaved split and equivalent unsplit wait/clone calls yield identical counts and durations, without double-counting; failed exec probes do not inflate successful Git execs. `make nvim-startup-strace` produces corrected aggregates, or the old evidence is explicitly marked invalid pending a permitted rerun.
  - **Constraints:** Keep `%process,%file` coverage and overhead/overlap caveats; do not infer wall time or CPU time from summed syscall durations. Use the existing profiling script/Make entry, not a new standalone one-off test file.
  - Verified (2026-11-22, base `b3b3096`; check): `make nvim-startup-strace V=1` → exit 0 (`ok: build`; target exit 0). Parser self-check output: `parser check: split wait4=1/100.000ms clone=1/2.000ms == unsplit wait4=1/100.000ms clone=1/2.000ms` and `parser check: successful Git execs=1, failed exec probes excluded=1, unmatched unfinished=1, unmatched resumed=1`. Corrected fresh run `build/startup-strace/20261122T071210-565053/`: six launches, 0 unmatched halves, per-launch medians successful Git execs 42, failed exec probes 0, `wait4` 44.5 calls / 1.8 ms blocking; fixture successful Git execs 27 each, worktree 57/63/57. Historical 2026-11-21 `by_syscall` durations, Git-exec column, `wait4` total and conclusion are explicitly invalidated and retained as provenance in [startup diagnosis](docs/STARTUP_DIAGNOSIS.md#syscall-and-subprocess-tracing-2026-11-21).
  - Done: `fbc18f5`.

- [x] **[P1] STARTUP-RV-04 — Stop subtracting deferred Git work from the open handler duration** (`scripts/measure_nvim_startup.py:50-67,111-115`, `run_sample`, `main`)
  - **Problem:** Deferred acquisition carries the originating trace ID after `handler_end`, but `git_total_ms` sums every Git event with that ID. `handler_minus_git_ms` subtracts this out-of-handler work from the earlier handler span and clamps negative results to zero, still labeling it a non-Git remainder. This metric ceased to be valid when opening became asynchronous.
  - **Why it matters:** Correctness of performance evidence: the summary can report zero or an understated remainder for reasons unrelated to an optimization, obscuring the very boundary changed by this branch.
  - **Change:** Remove the invalid per-sample remainder and its aggregate from current async measurement output; report handler and correlated Git durations independently with an explicit statement that their intervals differ. Do not invent replacement exclusive-time attribution. Update current explanatory wording in `docs/STARTUP_DIAGNOSIS.md` if necessary while leaving dated synchronous measurements clearly historical.
  - **Done when:** `make nvim-startup-check` retains the existing status/phase validation and cold/reopen distributions, but no current summary labels handler-minus-all-correlated-Git as a remainder or silently clamps it to zero. Record a raw trace showing deferred Git events after handler end as the reason for retiring this metric.
  - **Constraints:** Do not modify `scripts/measure_nvim_startup.lua`, change the measured visible-Head endpoint, or rewrite historical raw artifacts; no new timing threshold or speedup claim.
  - Done: `eaa7365`.
  - Verified: `make nvim-startup-check` @ `b546dfd` (working tree) → `ok: nvim-startup-check`; summary has independent `median_handler_ms`/`median_git_ms` with `profile_cost_intervals`, no `handler_minus_git_ms`; deferred `git_call` at 76.420 ms vs `handler_end` 60.281 ms in `build/startup-diagnosis/20261122T071530Z/fixture-1795331730257848226.trace.jsonl`.

Priority work before further feature execution. Earlier checked Review items are in the completion ledger; their priority labels are historical.

Binding release design (2026-11-21; inspected baseline `6ec2b57`, no committed delta): this series supersedes the earlier mixed-sysroot/Nix-package release proposal, not its dated evidence. Implement safe code even when hosted execution is unavailable; report evidence pending rather than asking for a local builder. No substantial local compilation, release build, portability or native acceptance: those run hosted only. Local verification is limited to small Python/JS/contract/actionlint/shellcheck checks through Make, serially. Proposed targets must exist before use. Do not run broad targets with hidden build prerequisites. No publication, tags, pushes, credentials, containers, infrastructure changes or hosted dispatch is authorized by these implementation tasks. **Authoritative acceptance amendment (2026-11-21):** Linux GNU x86_64 on Ubuntu 22.04 plus macOS arm64 and Intel are mandatory; Windows is informational only, outside every required matrix, readiness and publication condition until a future Windows architecture phase. Each platform's same build job runs the required debug-server tests then release build and server tests; only server tests run twice. Client/editor/ABI/runtime/package acceptance runs once against release output. Reuse same-job build cache while separating instrumented coverage outputs. Keep CI read-only source checks, release unprivileged build/test/package, and protected-default `workflow_run` publisher. Required graph is debug+release server tests → release acceptance/package/all-platform plus one strict VSIX gate → GitHub release → Marketplace/OpenVSX (and existing crate scope audit); any failed/skipped/cancelled/missing required job blocks GitHub release, GitHub-release failure blocks registry publication, and registry partial failure is reported as partial, never atomic success. Manual rehearsals never publish. Build the VSIX once with Node 24/frozen locked pnpm; install and publish those exact bytes. Trusted publishing/OIDC is required (`vsce --oidc`, `ovsx --trusted-publishing`), with `release-publish` and job-local id-token permission; no PAT fallback or credential-missing success. Preserve checksums, safe extraction, artifact/run/tag/source/version binding, actual release-binary smoke, ABI checks and OIDC privilege boundary. Simplify redundant repeated IDs/digests/ZIP API pagination fan-in and replicated promotion attestations: they are not security requirements by themselves. Keep hosted-only native build/fallback until hosted proof and human approval. No flake/lock/container/tool install/dispatch/tag/push/publish/settings changes.

**Queue reconciliation (2026-11-21):** the unchecked NREL-09–14 and NREL-17–20 Change/Done-when directives below are superseded for future implementation by the ordered replacement tickets in Ad hoc. Their dated implementation and verification records remain historical and accurate; do not tick those parents or infer hosted proof. NREL-15/16 retain only work not covered by the replacement tickets; update their dependency/order when implementing. T033 remains unchecked and human hosted-proof/approval is pending. The older checked Windows phase and its evidence remain history, but its Windows-native acceptance is deferred and informational; it is never a current release gate.

Protected: `flake.nix` and `flake.lock` MUST NOT be edited; preserve user-dirty `tests/test_magit_lsp.lua`, `.envrc`, `dist/`, `doc/tags`, `scripts/ci-local.sh`. VERIFIED interfaces: `flake.nix:45-69,137-138` supplies `release-native` (`mkShellNoCC`, Rust/Cargo/Git/make/Python) and `release-acceptance` (`mkShellNoCC`, Git/Python/Neovim/tmux/Node 24/pnpm, no Rust); package version already derives from Cargo at line 24. Native compilation uses Nix Rust with `/usr/bin/cc` or Apple native linker/SDK, never a Nix C wrapper/sysroot, musl, Zig or post-link patch. Retain Ubuntu 22.04 Rust 1.94.1 fallback until a separate nonpublishing experimental lane passes hosted proof. Signing is future work: any later signing must precede final hashing and acceptance.

Architecture: `ci.yml` owns read-only source checks reusable via `workflow_call` or shared Make targets. `release.yml` remains named `Release artifact build (no publication)`, accepts `v*` tag pushes and manual rehearsals, builds/accepts immutable candidates read-only. Separate `publish.yml` runs protected-default orchestration on `workflow_run: completed`, eligible successful push builds only; read-only validation precedes environment `release-publish` with job-local `contents: write`/`id-token: write`. No manual run-ID bypass or PAT fallback. Original tag/source comes from a run-bound manifest, never `head_branch`, inferred version or the publisher's default-branch `github.sha`. Mandatory outcomes must be success, not skip/cancel/inconclusive; Windows remains deferred/informational and gates nothing.

Inventory VERIFIED: reuse `scripts/check_release_pipeline.py:42-97` locked command/version handoff, `scripts/check_release_automation.py:682-843` checksum/run/repository/workflow/tag guards and existing self-tests, `tests/release/test_release.py:116-226` installer/archive tests, and the existing VSIX install scenario. Missing: target/provenance manifest, native-linker/ABI gates, no-build selectors, immutable VSIX handoff, strict aggregate and event-chain validation. Checker currently forbids `workflow_run` (line 151), binds to dispatch SHA (809-830), and retains PAT expectations (66); publisher already uses `vsce --oidc` and `ovsx --trusted-publishing` (157-164), but builds VSIX in validation (77-78). Do not reimplement these mechanisms from scratch. Historical NREL-01 and T030–T032 completion IDs remain intact; T033 owns outstanding release approval/evidence. Every acceptance below is `test: permanent` or `check: one-off`; hosted acceptance stays unclosed until proof. Assumption: unavailable hosted evidence does not authorize switching production or publishing; escalate design ambiguity to the reviewer, not a new human-blocked implementation ticket.

- [x] **[P0] Remove the hook's unsafe automatic working-copy overwrite** (`scripts/format.py:232-254,304-306`, `sync_working_copy`, `staged_fix`; `scripts/test_format.py:215-238`, `test_hook_syncs_matching_working_copy`)
  - **Problem:** VERIFIED in committed `402e4db`: sharing a descriptor does not make the read/compare/write atomic. An editor saving in place after `handle.read()` but before `write()`/`truncate()` loses its unstaged bytes; the caught `OSError` can also conceal a partial overwrite. `O_NOFOLLOW` protects only the final component (and falls back to zero when unavailable), so a parent directory replaced by a symlink after the earlier `staged_fix` check can redirect this new write outside the worktree. These are source-derived failure interleavings, not reproduced test results.
  - **Why it matters:** Correctness/data safety — an ordinary commit hook must not silently destroy concurrent user edits or modify a symlink target while claiming those cases are skipped.
  - **Change:** Restore index-only behavior for the ordinary staged hook: remove `sync_working_copy`, its invocation and the now-unused `stat` import. Adapt the matching-working-copy test to require the formatted index and unchanged working bytes; retain the partial-staging preservation regression. Leave explicit working-tree formatting as the existing separately requested operation. If automatic synchronization is mandatory, obtain a separate safety/design decision rather than replacing this with another stat/read check, advisory lock, or unconditional rename (none provides compare-and-swap against uncooperative editor saves).
  - **Done when:** `staged_fix` never opens a working-tree source for writing, matching and divergent working files remain byte-identical across the hook while staged blobs format, and `make format-test` passes. Record that automatic working-copy synchronization is deferred, not implemented safely by a same-descriptor check.
  - **Constraints:** Touch only `scripts/format.py`, `scripts/test_format.py` and this queue record. Preserve isolated staged formatting, partial-staging/index safeguards and explicit sweep behavior. No new synchronization framework or unsupported concurrent-edit safety claim.
  - Verified A1: `make format-test` → `ok: format-test`. `test_hook_leaves_matching_working_copy` (renamed from `test_hook_syncs_matching_working_copy`) asserts the index blob is `ok\n` while the working file stays byte-identical `bad\n` and `git status` reports ` M` for that path; `test_hook_preserves_partially_staged_working_edits` still asserts the index formats to `ok\n` with the working file left at `bad\nunstaged\n`.
  - Verified A2: `rg -n "sync_working_copy|O_RDWR|stat\.|import stat" scripts/format.py scripts/test_format.py` → no matches. `staged_fix` now ends at `update-index` plus one stderr diagnostic and never opens a working-tree source for writing; isolated snapshot formatting, the concurrent-index check, path/mode validation and the opt-in sweep are unchanged.
  - Verified A3: evidence recorded here and committed by explicit path (`140532b` source and test, then this queue record). Automatic working-copy synchronization is deferred: it is not implemented, and no same-descriptor read/compare/write, stat check, lock or rename is claimed safe against a concurrent uncooperative editor save.
  - Done: `140532b`.

- [x] **[P1] RV-06 — Bind release artifacts and publication to one verified source commit** (`.github/workflows/publish.yml:37-59,86-115`, `validate`, `publish`; `scripts/check_release_automation.py:401-422`, `validate_artifacts`)

Before any release creation, reject an existing version tag resolving to another commit; if tag creation is supported, explicitly target the verified SHA instead of the default branch. Do not execute downloaded binaries merely to establish provenance, rebuild the downloaded server, loosen checksum validation, or perform credential/tag/push/publication operations during implementation.

The checker's default mode now also runs fixture-driven self-tests, each asserting the intended guard message rather than any failure: `validate_run_metadata` accepts only the exact successful release-build identity and rejects the `wrong repository`, `fork head repository`, `missing repository`, `wrong workflow path`, `wrong workflow name`, `unfinished run`, `unsuccessful run` and `other source commit` fixtures; `release_target` accepts an absent tag (targeting the verified source SHA) and a tag at that SHA, rejecting `same version, other commit`, `tag lookup failure`, `tag lookup without a sha` and `tag lookup with invalid JSON`; `validate_artifacts` rejects `extra artifact file`, `tampered artifact checksum` and `missing artifact sidecar` while the exact validated pair passes. Workflow-wiring probes fail on the intended guard for: `publish workflow without a pinned validate checkout`, `... without a pinned publish checkout`, `... without run provenance`, `... using the VSIX before provenance`, `... handing off a dist glob`, `... uploading arbitrary release assets`, `... releasing without a target`, `... without a tag target check`, `... without artifact re-verification` and `... without frozen CLI dependencies` (the last one keeps the RV-04 prerequisite check covered). Neither the hosted provenance lookup nor any tag, push or publication was executed, so hosted evidence remains explicitly unclaimed. Not ticked: the Part 2/2 `make workflows-check` record and the RV06 tick stay with the parent.

Switching branches, moving a target ref, or editing files while the confirmation is open can therefore discard work or reset a different branch than the one presented; unlike existing destructive continuations, it has no fingerprint check. The destructive outcome is inferred from that argv/control flow, not a review-time execution.

On confirmation reject changed repository/HEAD attachment with a readable retry message, otherwise reset to the captured OID. Reuse existing fingerprint conventions, explicitly checking symbolic HEAD as well as its commit so switching between branches at the same commit is detected. Apply this shared continuation to `reset.hard`, `branch.reset` and `reset.branch`. Correct `GitRepo::reset_hard`'s false docstring claiming Git refuses loss of local changes.
  - **Done when:** Extend the existing hard-reset tests in `tests/commands/more.rs`: edits and same-OID branch switches during confirmation preserve exact post-change refs/index/worktree and report refusal; moving the entered target ref cannot silently change the approved target; unchanged confirmation still resets successfully. `make test-rust FILTER="--test commands"` passes.
